========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, thinking about it when I'm not trying to? No, not really. I mean, I really do my best to keep my mind occupied with other things, you know? I try to stay focused on the work I *can* do, or just, um, normal everyday stuff. 

I don't just sit around dwelling on it, I promise. I know that wouldn't be healthy or productive at all. I really try to just push it out of my head and look forward. I think I'm doing a pretty good job of just... keeping my thoughts in check. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just trying to stay focused on the positive and not letting my mind wander back there?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I know I mentioned a minute ago about trying to push it out of my head, but I wouldn't say I'm actively *avoiding* thinking about it, if that makes sense? Like, I'm not running away from the memory as if it terrifies me. 

I just... I choose not to dwell on it. I really don't want you to think I'm in denial or burying my head in the sand or anything like that. I just try to shift my focus back to my other clients and get my work done. I feel like if I spent all my time actively avoiding it, that would mean it's controlling me, right? 

I just want to face things maturely and move on. Choosing to focus on the present is the responsible thing to do, don't you think? I really hope I'm approaching this the right way.

  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh, no. I... I didn't mean it literally like that. "Outside my body" makes it sound a little too much like some kind of sci-fi movie or... or a hallucination, and I definitely don't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality. I'm very grounded, I promise. 

When I said watching from across the room, it was really more of a... a figure of speech, I guess? It's just a brief feeling of being disconnected, not like I'm literally floating around looking down at myself. I'm definitely still in my own skin. I just... I really hope I didn't give you the wrong impression with how I explained it before. I'm trying my best to describe it properly, so... does that clarify things a bit?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I suppose disconnected is a really good way to put it. Like I was trying to explain a minute ago, it's really just those moments where my own house or the office suddenly feels completely unreal, almost like a movie set or something. And I get that strange sense of detachment from myself, like that feeling of watching myself from across the room that I brought up earlier. 

But it's very brief, and I always stay perfectly professional through it, I swear. I really hope I'm making sense and not sounding too unstable. It's just a little embarrassing to admit to having these weird disconnected moments when I'm normally so focused and grounded. That's a fairly normal reaction to stress, though, isn't it?

Oh, um... I haven't really kept a tally or anything like that. It's... it's really not that often, I promise. Maybe just, I don't know, a handful of times since that whole thing happened with the client? It's definitely not a daily occurrence.
